In 1967 it joined forces with [John Tyndall](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/John_Tyndall_\(politician\)) and the remnants of Chesterton's League of Empire Loyalists to form Britain's largest far-right organization, the [National Front](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/British_National_Front) (NF).[^cite_note-453] The BNP and the NF supported extreme [loyalism](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ulster_loyalism) in [Northern Ireland](21265.md), and attracted Conservative Party members who had become disillusioned after [Harold Macmillan](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Harold_Macmillan) had recognized the right to independence of the African colonies and had criticized [Apartheid](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/South_Africa_under_apartheid) in South Africa.[^cite_note-454]

Some Northern Irish [loyalist paramilitaries](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Loyalist_paramilitaries) have links with far-right and [neo-Nazi](54361.md) groups in Britain, including [Combat 18](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Combat_18),[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEGoodrick-Clarke200345-455][^cite_note-456] the [British National Socialist Movement](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/British_Movement)[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEGoodrick-Clarke200340–41-457] and the NF.[^cite_note-458] In 2004, *[The Guardian](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/The_Guardian)* reported that loyalist paramilitaries had been responsible for numerous racist attacks in loyalist areas.[^cite_note-459] During the 1970s, the NF's rallies became a regular feature of British politics. Election results remained strong in a few [working-class](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Working-class) urban areas, with a number of local council seats won, but the party never came anywhere near winning representation in parliament.



[British National Party](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/British_National_Party) (BNP) vote share in the 2010 UK general election

Since the 1970s, the NF's support has been in decline whilst [Nick Griffin](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Nick_Griffin) and the current [British National Party](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/British_National_Party) (BNP) grew in popularity. Around the turn of the 21st century, the BNP won a number of council seats. At its peak in the late 2000s, the party had 54 local council seats, one seat in the [London Assembly](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/London_Assembly), two seats in the [European Parliament](9581.md), and were the official opposition in the [Barking and Dagenham London Borough Council](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Barking_and_Dagenham_London_Borough_Council). The party received almost a million votes in the [2009 European Parliament elections](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2009_European_Parliament_elections), and contested the majority of UK parliamentary seats in the [2010 general election](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2010_United_Kingdom_general_election). The party's membership was 12,632 and its financial resources were an estimated £1,983,947.[^cite_note-Vandenhoeck-2011-54] The BNP would record their highest ever vote in a general election with half a million or 1.9% of the popular vote. This is the highest ever vote for a British [Far-right](3884621.md) party.

By the early 2010s the BNP saw its support and membership quickly collapse due to internal divisions caused by a disappointing performance in the 2010 elections. Griffin was ousted as leader in 2014 after losing his European Parliament seat, and since then the party has been in terminal decline under the leadership of [Adam Walker](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Adam_Walker_\(British_politician\)).

A number of breakaway groups have been established by former members of the BNP, such as [Britain First](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Britain_First) by ex-councillor [Paul Golding](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Paul_Golding), the [British Democrats](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/British_Democrats) by ex-MEP and [leadership candidate](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2011_British_National_Party_leadership_election) [Andrew Brons](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Andrew_Brons), as well as [Patriotic Alternative](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Patriotic_Alternative) by [Mark Collett](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Mark_Collett). [UK Independence Party](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/UK_Independence_Party) (UKIP) leader [Nigel Farage](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Nigel_Farage) claimed that his party absorbed much of the BNP's former voters during their electoral peak in the early 2010s.[^cite_note-460] The party was accused of shifting towards far-right, anti-Islam politics under the leadership of [Paul Nuttall](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Paul_Nuttall) and [Gerard Batten](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Gerard_Batten) during its decline in the late 2010s. Anti-Islam activist and former UKIP [leadership candidate](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2017_UK_Independence_Party_leadership_election) [Anne Marie Waters](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Anne_Marie_Waters) established the far-right [For Britain Movement](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/For_Britain_Movement), which gained a small number of ex-BNP councillors. It was deregistered in 2022, and subsequently a large portion of prominent far-right activists began coalescing around the British Democrats, which (following UKIP's loss of its few councillors on 4 May 2023, leaving it with only a few parish and town councillors) quickly established itself as the UK's only far-right party with any electoral representation.

### Oceania

#### Australia

Main article: [Far-right politics in Australia](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Far-right_politics_in_Australia)



Captain [Francis de Groot](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Francis_de_Groot) declares the [Sydney Harbour Bridge](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Sydney_Harbour_Bridge) open in March 1932.

Coming to prominence in [Sydney](27862.md) with the formation of the [New Guard](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/New_Guard) (1931) and the [Centre Party](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Centre_Party_\(New_South_Wales\)) (1933), the far right has played a part in Australian political discourse since the second world war.[^cite_note-461] These [proto-fascist](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Proto-fascist) groups were [monarchist](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Monarchist), anti-communist and [authoritarian](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Authoritarian) in nature. Early far-right groups were followed by the explicitly fascist [Australia First Movement](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Australia_First_Movement) (1941).[^cite_note-naa-462][^cite_note-NLA-463] The far right in Australia went on to acquire more explicitly racial connotations during the 1960s and 1970s, morphing into self-proclaimed [Nazi](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Nazism), [fascist](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Fascism) and [antisemitic](1078.md) movements, organizations that opposed non-white and non-Christian immigration such as the [neo-Nazi](54361.md) [National Socialist Party of Australia](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/National_Socialist_Party_of_Australia) (1967) and the militant white supremacist group [National Action](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/National_Action_\(Australia\)) (1982).[^cite_note-464][^cite_note-Saleam-465][^cite_note-auslander-466]

Since the 1980s, the term has mainly been used to describe those who express the wish to preserve what they perceive to be [Judeo-Christian](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Judeo-Christian), [Anglo-Australian](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Anglo-Australian) culture and those who campaign against [Aboriginal land rights](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Aboriginal_land_rights), [multiculturalism](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Multiculturalism_in_Australia), [immigration](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Immigration_in_Australia) and [asylum seekers](40011807.md). Since 2001, Australia has seen the development of modern neo-Nazi, [neo-fascist](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Neo-fascist) or [alt-right](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Alt-right) groups such as the [True Blue Crew](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/True_Blue_Crew), the [United Patriots Front](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/United_Patriots_Front), [Fraser Anning's Conservative National Party](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Fraser_Anning's_Conservative_National_Party) and the [Antipodean Resistance](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Antipodean_Resistance).[^cite_note-Guardian-467]

#### New Zealand

Main article: [Far-right politics in New Zealand](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Far-right_politics_in_New_Zealand)

A small number of far-right organizations have existed in New Zealand since World War II, including the Conservative Front, the [New Zealand National Front](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/New_Zealand_National_Front) and the National Democrats Party.[^cite_note-Stuff.co.nz_2389206-468][^cite_note-NZ_Herald_3603497-469] Far-right parties in New Zealand lack significant support, with their protests often dwarfed by counter protest.[^cite_note-470] After the [Christchurch mosque shootings](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Christchurch_mosque_shootings) in 2019, the National Front "publicly shut up shop"[^cite_note-471] and largely went underground like other far-right groups.[^cite_note-472]

#### Fiji

##### Nationalist Vanua Tako Lavo Party

Main article: [Nationalist Vanua Tako Lavo Party](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Nationalist_Vanua_Tako_Lavo_Party)

The Nationalist Vanua Tako Lavo Party was a far-right political party which advocated Fijian [ethnic nationalism](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ethnic_nationalism).[^cite_note-473] In 2009, party leader [Iliesa Duvuloco](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Iliesa_Duvuloco) was arrested for breaching the military regime's emergency laws by distributing pamphlets calling for an uprising against the military regime.[^cite_note-474] In January 2013, the military regime introduced regulations that essentially de-registered the party.[^cite_note-475][^cite_note-476]

### Pan-national

Main article: [Pan-nationalism](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Pan-nationalism)

#### European Union

The development of a [Pan-European identity](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Pan-European_identity) among far-right members of the European parliament has been claimed.[^cite_note-g182-477]

#### Islamic extremism

Some [Islamic extremists](8734632.md) view Islam superior to all other ideologies and [non-Muslims](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Kafir) as inferior.[^cite_note-478] Some Islamic extremism can be seen as far-right,[^cite_note-k356-228] and can have some social acceptance in some countries.[^cite_note-c820-262] [Dhimmi](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Dhimmi) refers to the inferior status of non-Muslims in some historic Islamic states.[^cite_note-Campo-479]

## Online

Main article: [Far-right usage of the internet](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Far-right_usage_of_the_internet)

A number of far-right internet pages and forums are focused on and frequented by the far right. These include Stormfront and Iron March.

Far-right internet movements gained popularity and notoriety online in 2012, and this has not stopped.[^cite_note-tt-480] In the United States, they gained many followers during the [2016 presidential election](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2016_United_States_presidential_election), the time after the election during Obama's last months in office in 2016, and in 2017.[^cite_note-tt-480]

### Stormfront

Main article: [Stormfront (website)](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Stormfront_\(website\))

Stormfront is the oldest and most prominent [neo-Nazi](54361.md) website,[^cite_note-481] described by the Southern Poverty Law Center and other media organizations as the "murder capital of the internet".[^cite_note-482] In August 2017, Stormfront was taken offline for just over a month when its [registrar](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Domain_name_registrar) seized its domain name due to complaints that it promoted [hatred](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Hatred) and that some of its members were linked to [murder](20171.md). The [Lawyers' Committee for Civil Rights Under Law](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Lawyers'_Committee_for_Civil_Rights_Under_Law) claimed credit for the action after advocating for Stormfront's web host, [Network Solutions](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Network_Solutions), to enforce its Terms of Service agreement which prohibits users from using its services to incite violence.[^cite_note-483]

### Iron March

Main article: [Iron March](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Iron_March)

Iron March was a fascist web forum founded in 2011 by Russian nationalist Alexander "Slavros" Mukhitdinov. An unknown individual uploaded a database of Iron March users to the [Internet Archive](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Internet_Archive) in November 2019 and multiple neo-Nazi users were identified, including an [ICE](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/U.S._Immigration_and_Customs_Enforcement) detention center captain and several active members of the [United States Armed Forces](32212.md).[^cite_note-TheGuardian-Nov2019-LeakIdentify-484][^cite_note-485] As of mid 2018, the [Southern Poverty Law Center](27164.md) linked Iron March to nearly 100 murders.[^cite_note-Vice-Mar2018-WaveOfTerror-486][^cite_note-TheGuardian-Nov2019-LeakIdentify-484] Mukhitdinov remained a murky figure at the time of the leaks.[^cite_note-Bellingcat-Dec2019-ExplosingNetworks-487]

### Terrorgram

Main article: [Terrorgram](72327036.md)

The Terrorgram community on [Telegram](41501305.md) is a network of Telegram channels and accounts that subscribe to and promote militant [accelerationism](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Accelerationism). Terrorgram channels are [neofascist](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Neofascist) in ideology, and regularly share instructions and manuals on how to carry out acts of racially motivated violence and anti-government, anti-authority terrorism.[^cite_note-GNET-488] In 2021, the Institute for Strategic Dialogue (ISD), an international think-tank, exposed more than two hundred neo-Nazi pro-terrorism telegram channels that make up the Terrorgram network, many of which contained instructions to build weapons and bombs.[^cite_note-Bratislava-489][^cite_note-490][^cite_note-491]

## Right-wing terrorism

Main article: [Right-wing terrorism](5210960.md)



From 2013 through 2022 75% of extremist killings in the U.S. were caused by right-wing perpetrators.

[^cite_note-AFL_20230222-492]

&#x20;From 2022 through 2024, all 61 political killings were committed by right-wing extremists.

[^cite_note-AP_20250914-190]



The 1980 [Bologna massacre](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Bologna_massacre) by [Nuclei Armati Rivoluzionari](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Nuclei_Armati_Rivoluzionari)

Right-wing terrorism is [terrorism](30636.md) motivated by a variety of far right ideologies and beliefs, including anti-communism, [neo-fascism](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Neo-fascism), [neo-Nazism](54361.md), [racism](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Racism), [xenophobia](34141.md) and [opposition to immigration](6491626.md). This type of terrorism has been sporadic, with little or no international cooperation.[^cite_note-Aubrey,_p._45-493] Modern right-wing terrorism first appeared in western Europe in the 1980s and it first appeared in Eastern Europe following the [dissolution of the Soviet Union](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Dissolution_of_the_Soviet_Union).[^cite_note-Moghadam,_pp._57-58-494]

Right-wing terrorists aim to overthrow governments and replace them with nationalist or fascist-oriented governments.[^cite_note-Aubrey,_p._45-493] The core of this movement includes [neo-fascist skinheads](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/White_power_skinhead), far-right [hooligans](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Football_hooliganism), youth sympathizers and intellectual guides who believe that the state must rid itself of foreign elements in order to protect rightful citizens. However, they usually lack a rigid ideology.[^cite_note-Moghadam,_pp._57-58-494]

According to [Cas Mudde](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Cas_Mudde), far-right terrorism and violence in the West have been generally perpetrated in recent times by individuals or groups of individuals "who have at best a peripheral association" with politically relevant organizations of the far right. Nevertheless, Mudde follows, "in recent years far-right violence has become more planned, regular, and lethal, as terrorists attacks in [Christchurch](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Christchurch_mosque_shootings) (2019), [Pittsburgh](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Pittsburgh_synagogue_shooting) (2018), and [Norway](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2011_Norway_attacks) (2011) show."[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEMudde2019\[\[Category\:Wikipedia_articles_needing_page_number_citations_from_August_2021\]\]<sup_class="noprint_Inline-Template_"_style="white-space\:nowrap;">\[<i>\[\[Wikipedia\:Citing_sources|<span_title="This_citation_requires_a_reference_to_the_specific_page_or_range_of_pages_in_which_the_material_appears. (August_2021)">page needed</span>\]\]</i>\]</sup>-38]

[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEMudde201912-32]: &#x20;[Mudde 2019](#CITEREFMudde2019), p. 12: "The *extreme right* rejects the essence of democracy, that is, popular sovereignty and majority rule. The most infamous example of the extreme right is fascism, which brought to power German *Führer* Adolf Hitler and Italian *Duce* Benito Mussolini, and was responsible for the most destructive war in world history. The *radical right* accepts the essence of democracy, but opposes fundamental elements of liberal democracy, most notably minority rights, rule of law, and separation of powers. Both subgroups oppose the postwar liberal democratic consensus, but in fundamentally different ways. While the extreme right is revolutionary, the radical right is more reformist. In essence, the radical right trusts the power of the people, the extreme right does not."

[doi](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Doi_\(identifier\)):[10.1080/10714839.1998.11725657](https://doi.org/10.1080%2F10714839.1998.11725657). the military's growing frustration over the limitations placed upon its counterinsurgency operations by democratic institutions, coupled with the growing inability of civilian politicians to deal with the spiraling economic crisis and the expansion of the Shining Path, prompted a group of military officers to devise a coup plan in the late 1980s. The plan called for the dissolution of Peru's civilian government, military control over the state, and total elimination of armed opposition groups. The plan, developed in a series of documents known as the "Plan Verde," outlined a strategy for carrying out a military coup in which the armed forces would govern for 15 to 20 years and radically restructure state-society relations along neoliberal lines.

[^cite_note-SPLCenter-177]: &#x20;\
    &#x20;•  [Zaitchik, Alexander](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Alexander_Zaitchik) (19 October 2006). ["The National Socialist Movement Implodes"](https://www.splcenter.org/fighting-hate/intelligence-report/2006/national-socialist-movement-implodes). *SPLCenter.org*. [Montgomery, Alabama](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Montgomery,_Alabama): [Southern Poverty Law Center](27164.md). [Archived](https://web.archive.org/web/20150919115024/https://www.splcenter.org/fighting-hate/intelligence-report/2006/national-socialist-movement-implodes) from the original on 19 September 2015. Retrieved 28 December 2020. The party's problems began last June, when Citizens Against Hate discovered that [NSM](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/National_Socialist_Movement_\(United_States\))'s [Tulsa](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Tulsa,_Oklahoma) post office box was shared by [The Joy of Satan Ministry](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Joy_of_Satan_Ministries), in which the wife of NSM chairman emeritus Clifford Herrington is High Priestess. \[...] Within NSM ranks, meanwhile, a bitter debate was sparked over the propriety of Herrington's Joy of Satan connections. \[...] [Schoep](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Jeff_Schoep) moved ahead with damage-control operations by nudging chairman emeritus Herrington from his position under the cover of "attending to personal matters". But it was too late to stop NSM Minister of Radio and Information Michael Blevins, aka Vonbluvens, from following [White](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Bill_White_\(neo-Nazi\)) out of the party, citing disgust with Herrington's Joy of Satan ties. "Satanism," declared Blevins in his resignation letter, "affects the whole prime directive guiding the \[NSM] – SURVIVAL OF THE WHITE RACE." \[...] NSM was now a Noticeably Smaller Movement, one trailed in extremist circles by a strong whiff of Satanism and related charges of [sexual impropriety](457212.md) associated with Joy of Satan initiation rites and curiously strong teen recruitment efforts.\
    &#x20;•  ["National Socialist Movement"](https://www.splcenter.org/fighting-hate/extremist-files/group/national-socialist-movement). *SPLCenter.org*. [Montgomery, Alabama](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Montgomery,_Alabama): [Southern Poverty Law Center](27164.md). 2020. [Archived](https://web.archive.org/web/20150908010431/https://www.splcenter.org/fighting-hate/extremist-files/group/national-socialist-movement) from the original on 8 September 2015. Retrieved 28 December 2020. The NSM has had its share of movement scandal. In July 2006, it was rocked by revelations that co-founder and chairman emeritus Cliff Herrington's wife was the "High Priestess" of the Joy of Satan Ministry, and that her satanic church shared an address with the [Tulsa, Okla.](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Tulsa,_Oklahoma), NSM chapter. The exposure of Herrington's wife's Satanist connections caused quite a stir, particularly among those NSM members who adhered to a racist (and heretical) variant of Christianity, [Christian Identity](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Christian_Identity). Before the dust settled, both Herringtons were forced out of NSM. [Bill White](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Bill_White_\(neo-Nazi\)), the neo-Nazi group's energetic spokesman, also quit, taking several NSM officials with him to create a new group, the American National Socialist Workers Party.

[Archived](https://web.archive.org/web/20170922205137/https://www.adl.org/education/resources/profiles/national-socialist-movement) from the original on 22 September 2017. Retrieved 28 December 2020.

[^cite_note-Upchurch_2021-179]: &#x20;Upchurch, H. E. (22 December 2021). Cruickshank, Paul; Hummel, Kristina (eds.). ["The Iron March Forum and the Evolution of the "Skull Mask" Neo-Fascist Network"](https://ctc.usma.edu/wp-content/uploads/2021/12/CTC-SENTINEL-102021.pdf) (PDF). *[CTC Sentinel](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/CTC_Sentinel)*. **14** (10). [West Point, New York](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/West_Point,_New_York): [Combating Terrorism Center](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Combating_Terrorism_Center): 27–37. [Archived](https://web.archive.org/web/20211227044425/https://ctc.usma.edu/wp-content/uploads/2021/12/CTC-SENTINEL-102021.pdf) (PDF) from the original on 27 December 2021. Retrieved 19 January 2022. The skull mask network's ideology is a political-religious hybrid based in large part on the work of the philosopher [Julius Evola](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Julius_Evola). Evola mixed [fascism](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Fascism) with "Traditionalism," a syncretic 20th century religious movement that combines [Hermetic](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Hermeticism) [occultism](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Occultism) with the [Hindu doctrine](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Hinduism) of [cyclical time](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Hindu_cosmology#Time) and a belief in a now-lost primordial [European paganism](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Paganism). Adherents of this blend of doctrines, which can be termed "Traditionalist fascism" believe that a [caste-based](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Caste_system), [racially pure](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Purity_of_race) ["organic" society](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Organicism) will be restored after what they believe to be an ongoing age of corruption, the [Kali Yuga](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Kali_Yuga), is swept away in an apocalyptic war, and that it is their role to hasten the end of the Kali Yuga by generating chaos and violence.

[^cite_note-7]: &#x20;Many far-right politics, there are cases where [ethnic nationalist](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ethnic_nationalist) or [nativist](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Nativism_\(politics\)) elements are prominent, but not all ethnic nationalism does not appear only in political right;<sup>[\[1\]](#cite_note-2)</sup><sup>[\[2\]](#cite_note-3)</sup><sup>[\[3\]](#cite_note-Yang_2025-4)</sup> some far-right ideologies are characterized by [state nationalism](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/State_nationalism)—a form of authoritarian [civic nationalism](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Civic_nationalism)—marked by [imperialist](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Imperialist) or [anti-leftist](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Anti-leftist) elements (ex: [Integral nationalism](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Integral_nationalism), *[Kokkashugi](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Kokkashugi)*, [Putinism](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Putinism), etc.).<sup>[\[3\]](#cite_note-Yang_2025-4)</sup><sup>[\[4\]](#cite_note-5)</sup><sup>[\[5\]](#cite_note-6)</sup>

[^cite_note-272]: &#x20;Ethnic (*minjok*) nationalist elements are more prominent in the South Korean [nationalist-left](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Left-wing_nationalism_by_country#South_Korea) combined with [anti-imperialism](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Anti-imperialism) ([anti-Japanese sentiment](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Anti-Japanese_sentiment_in_Korea), [right of return](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Right_of_return) of the [diaspora](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Korean_diaspora) and [policy of reconciliation with North Korea](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Sunshine_Policy)) than in the South Korean far-right.<sup>[\[3\]](#cite_note-Yang_2025-4)</sup><sup>[\[264\]](#cite_note-stillunloved-268)</sup><sup>[\[265\]](#cite_note-269)</sup> Unlike the right-wing ethno-nationalists of the 20th century, modern left-wing *minjok* nationalists oppose the "pure blood" theory.<sup>[\[266\]](#cite_note-270)</sup><sup>[\[267\]](#cite_note-271)</sup>

[^cite_note-279]: &#x20;Among them, the mainstream right or centre-right are [Republic of China nationalists](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Republic_of_China_nationalism).

[^cite_note-31]: &#x20;[Mudde 2002](#CITEREFMudde2002), p. 12: "Simply stated, the difference between radicalism and extremism is that the former is *verfassungswidrig* (opposed to the constitution), whereas the latter is *verfassungsfeindlich* (hostile towards the constitution). This difference is of the utmost practical importance for the political parties involved, as extremist parties are extensively watched by the (federal and state) *Verfassungsschutz* and can even be banned, whereas radical parties are free from this control."

[^cite_note-33]: &#x20;[Mudde 2002](#CITEREFMudde2002), p. 13: "All in all, most definitions of (whatever) populism do not differ that much in content from the definitions of right-wing extremism. \[...] When the whole range of different terms and definitions used in the field is surveyed, there are striking similarities, with the various terms often being used synonymously and without any clear intention. Only a few authors, most notably those working within the extremist-theoretical tradition, clearly distinguish between the various terms."
